./docs/performance_counters/performance_counters.md:1:# Tensix Performance Counters Guide
./docs/performance_counters/performance_counters.md:3:This guide documents the interface for controlling and reading Tensix hardware performance counters. It covers how to configure and read them from C++ and Python, which useful derived metrics you can compute, and how to test both the interface and the metrics. It consolidates the hardware documentation and aligns it with the implemented interfaces found in this repository.
./docs/performance_counters/performance_counters.md:7:Tensix cores contain five hardware performance counter banks (also referred to as "groups" in some hardware documentation). This guide consistently uses the term "bank".
./docs/performance_counters/performance_counters.md:155:**Note on HF cycles:** "HF" refers to "Half-Fidelity" cycles. In Tensix math operations, fidelity settings control precision vs speed tradeoffs. Higher fidelity (HiFi) uses more cycles per operation for better precision; lower fidelity (LoFi) uses fewer cycles. The HF cycle counts indicate how many half-fidelity-equivalent cycles the instruction consumed.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:1:# Getting started with Tensix Core and Low-level Kernels
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:5:Low-Level Kernels (LLKs) form the foundational layer in the Tenstorrent software stack. LLKs directly drive hardware to perform matrix and vector operations that are fundamental to machine learning computations. This document provides an overview of Tensix Core functions.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:7:## Tensix Core
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:9:Tenstorrent chips are architected as a matrix of Tensix Cores. Figure 1 shows the top-level diagram of a Tensix Core:
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:12:  <img src="images/tensix_core.png" alt="Top-level diagram of Tensix Core" width="600" /><br/>
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:13:  <em>Figure 1: Top-level diagram of Tensix Core</em><br><br>
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:16:Tensix Core consists of four major parts:
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:19:2. Two Routers - Manage data movement between Tensix Cores and DRAM across the chip.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:22:   * Three TRISCs - Control the Tensix Engine through specialized Tensix instructions.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:23:4. Tensix Engine - Hardware accelerator controlled by TRISC processors, optimized for matrix computations essential to AI/ML operations.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:25:LLKs primarily operate by transferring data to and from L1 memory, and programming the Tensix Engine to perform various operations on the stored data.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:27:## Tensix Engine
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:29:The Tensix Engine is a multi-threaded, single-issue, in-order processor that uses a custom instruction set architecture known as the Tensix ISA. It is run by three concurrent threads, each managed by a dedicated TRISC processor. The Tensix ISA differs from the RISC-V instruction set; instructions are issued by RISC-V cores and then executed by the Tensix Engine.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:31:Figure 2 represents a simplified top-level architecture of the Tensix Engine:
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:33:  <img src="images/tensix_engine.png" alt="Top-level architecture of Tensix Engine" width="500" /><br/>
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:34:  <em>Figure 2: Top-level architecture of Tensix Engine</em><br><br>
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:38:Figure 3 illustrates the common data flow within the Tensix Engine:
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:48:  <img src="images/tensix_core_data_flow.png" alt="Data flow inside Tensix Engine" width="400" /><br/>
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:49:  <em>Figure 3: Data flow inside Tensix Core</em>
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:64:2. Tile order - A specialized storage format optimized for operations performed by the Tensix Cores.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:90:The Tensix hardware can use a limited number of bits for multiplication operations. To utilize all the bits from the datums of a particular data format, several multiplication phases might be required to achieve full precision.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:91:[Math fidelity](https://docs.tenstorrent.com/pybuda/latest/dataformats.html) is a key concept in achieving computational accuracy. Tensix Cores support up to four fidelity phases depending on the data format. Tenstorrent allows developers to choose the fidelity level for some operations, balancing accuracy requirements with performance for their specific application.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:95:The Unpacker is a DMA engine that transfers data between L1 memory and source/destination registers. The Tensix architecture implements two unpackers:
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:105:Unpackers feature hardware-accelerated data format conversion through specialized components called gaskets, eliminating software overhead. TRISC0 controls both unpackers through a subset of the Tensix instruction set.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:116:The Floating Point Unit (FPU) serves as the primary computational engine within the Tensix Engine, executing the majority of mathematical operations.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:131:The FPU is controlled through a dedicated set of Tensix instructions issued by TRISC1.
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:168:Table 1 contains all unpack operations currently implemented for Tensix:
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:182:Table 2 contains all math operations currently implemented for Tensix:
./docs/llk/l2/top_level_overview.md:195:Table 3 contains all pack operations currently implemented for Tensix:
./docs/llk/l1/intro.md:5:Each Tenstorrent chip consists of a grid of Tensix Cores that work together to run advanced ML models.
./docs/llk/l1/intro.md:7:These kernels enable AI model operation on Tensix cores at high performance. They act as the first software layer above the hardware and provide direct access to its computational capabilities.
./docs/llk/l1/intro.md:10:![Tensix Core to Chip](images/LLK-L1-Tensix-1-25_official.png)
./docs/llk/l1/intro.md:14:LLKs control the Tensix Engine, the fundamental computational unit of the Tensix Core, using the custom Tensix instruction set (Tensix ISA) extensions. LLKs also simplify usage of the Tensix ISA, allowing operations to be performed with maximum efficiency and fully utilizing the hardware's parallelism and optimized compute resources.
./docs/llk/l1/intro.md:16:As the foundational software layer, LLKs provide a direct interface for higher-level applications to harness the full power of the Tensix core, encapsulating hardware specifics into software primitives. They enable low-level tasks like data movement and mathematical operations to be executed at peak performance.
./docs/llk/l1/intro.md:23:- [**TT-Forge™**](https://github.com/tenstorrent/tt-forge): A compiler that bridges machine learning frameworks with Tenstorrent hardware, using LLKs to optimize the execution of high-level models on Tensix cores.
./docs/llk/l1/intro.md:27:![LLK in Tenstorrent Stack](images/LLK-L1-Tensix-3-25_official.png)
./docs/llk/l1/intro.md:31:The LLK repository provides the necessary resources for developing and testing kernels. It also includes a testing environment which validates that the LLKs function correctly on platforms like Wormhole and Blackhole. By targeting a single Tensix core, LLKs are optimized for high-performance execution under real-world workloads, and ensure that developers can fully leverage the hardware’s capabilities. The LLK repository has its own independent CI and test infrastructure, making it easier for developers to contribute.

./docs/tests/getting_started.md:491:which will run tests on 10 Tensix tiles on Wormhole and Blackhole in parallel.
./README.md:74:   Provides a high-level look at the Tensix Core and Tensix Engine architecture, including data organization for efficient LLK usage and operations supported by LLKs. This document is not tied to any specific chip generation (such as Wormhole) and is aimed at engineers and technical readers who want to understand the general architecture and capabilities.
